The Rockets have jumped out to a historic start on the offensive side of the ball, and a big reason has been the leap of fourth-year guard Amen Thompson. The former Overtime Elite star has become one of the league’s best two-way players, averaging an impressive 16.4 PPG while guarding the opposing team’s best player on most nights. He enters Wednesday’s contest against the Suns having scored 20 or more points in three of his last four games, while continuing to fill up the box score.

Thompson and the Rockets face Phoenix for the second time this season after dropping the first meeting earlier this month. The 23-year-old was one of the few positives in that defeat, scoring 17 points with 8 rebounds and 4 blocks. Houston now looks for revenge against a Western Conference foe that surprisingly stumped them defensively last time out. Phoenix ranks 18th in the NBA, conceding 115.0 PPG, with 47.3 of them coming inside the paint. An elite slasher like Thompson should dominate this matchup given the Suns’ lack of rim protection.

Moving to the SG spot, we are rolling with Milwaukee Bucks rookie Brayden Burries. The former Arizona standout has quickly found his footing in the NBA and been a bright spot in a bleak start to the season for the Bucks. He is averaging only 11.4 PPG for the season, but a recent hot stretch has seen him score in double digits in five straight games, including a 26-point outburst two games ago. His value does not rely on scoring alone, as the 21-year-old has shown an impressive knack for attacking the glass despite his 6’4″ frame.

Burries’ 5.72 rebounds per game rank in the top 10 among SGs, and that number has been climbing as he navigates his rookie season. He has 18 rebounds across his last two games, including his second career double-double in the Bucks’ most recent victory on Monday. He now looks to keep the momentum going against one of the league’s poorest defenses. Brooklyn enters Wednesday with just three wins, conceding 120.3 PPG, the 27th most in the league. The Nets also rank second to last at just 40.6 rebounds per game, so Burries should have plenty of chances to rack up boards.

We are taking advantage of DraftKings’ positional flexibility for our SF on Wednesday, with Minnesota’s impressive young wing Terrence Shannon Jr. slotting into our lineup for just $4,700. The 26-year-old, often used as a SG, has developed into one of the best bench scorers in the league in just his third season. He enters Wednesday’s contest against the shorthanded Oklahoma City Thunder averaging 13.1 PPG and is in the midst of one of the best scoring stretches of his career, with 10 or more points in six straight games, including a 34-point outburst against the Trail Blazers last Monday.

The Wolves’ high-volume scorer should continue to see ample playing time with superstar SG Anthony Edwards injured. Shannon is often the first player off the bench for head coach Chris Finch and plays 23.1 minutes per game, the sixth most on the team. Unlike Burries, the bulk of Shannon’s value relies on scoring, as he averages just 3.3 rebounds and 3.0 assists per game. Even so, he should find plenty of areas to attack a Thunder side decimated by injuries and nowhere near the defensive team it was at full strength over the past few seasons.

Knicks wing Landry Shamet would not be considered a PF in many cases, though we are slotting him there in this lineup regardless. The veteran three-point marksman has become a staple in the rotation of the defending NBA champions and ranks fourth on the team at 11.4 PPG. Shamet moved into the starting lineup with OG Anunoby missing the last two games and should remain there against the Cavaliers on Wednesday evening thanks to the scoring punch he has provided. The 29-year-old has scored 37 points in those two games combined and is off to a blazing start shooting the ball.

Shamet is shooting an impressive 44.2% from beyond the arc on 5.6 attempts per game. He has hit nine threes in his last two games as a starter and has scored in double digits in each of the five games during New York’s current win streak. He now looks to continue that hot stretch against a Cavaliers side that has not been particularly good defensively, especially on the perimeter. Wednesday marks the third meeting between these Eastern Conference powerhouses, with Shamet hitting five threes off the bench across the first two. Having played 30 minutes in his last three games, he should see more healthy scoring volume as one of the league’s hottest shooters.

Another Knicks reserve who has stepped in and provided solid contributions is veteran big man Andre Drummond. The 33-year-old has had some inconsistent runs in the rotation in his first 17 games as a Knick but has seen the floor more steadily over the past few games. With starting C Karl-Anthony Towns questionable for Wednesday, Drummond could start for the second time in three games. The former 76er stepped into the starting five on Sunday with Towns dealing with an illness and finished with a near double-double: 7 points, 13 rebounds, and 4 blocks in 23 minutes.

The NBA’s all-time leader in team rebound percentage continues to be a force on the glass in his 15th season. He has back-to-back games with 10-plus rebounds entering Wednesday and 35 total rebounds across his last four games despite playing just 17.0 minutes per contest in that stretch. Drummond’s value will depend heavily on Towns’ status, though he has had solid outings off the bench this season as well. Bulls guard Josh Giddey is having a quietly strong season that is being overlooked because of his team’s record. The former Thunder draft pick has developed into one of the league’s best playmakers early in his sixth season, and the 24-year-old has taken the lead in assists per game after back-to-back double-digit assist games. The Australian is averaging 10.4 PPG and 9.35 assists per contest while adding a solid 6.12 rebounds per game. Whether or not his impact on winning can be debated, he is undoubtedly one of the best fantasy options because of his ability to stuff a box score.

The former sixth-overall pick made headlines recently with a 22-assist night in an overtime thriller against the Nuggets on Sunday. He is one of 43 players in NBA history to reach 22 assists in a game and followed it up with 11 dimes in an upset of the Cavaliers on Monday. With both SG Norman Powell and PF Caleb Wilson out, Giddey continues to serve as the primary ball handler nearly any time he is on the floor, and his ability to break down defenses has been on full display.

Rockets big man Alperen Sengun has developed into one of the best offensive bigs in the league and leads the No. 1 offense in points (21.9), rebounds (9.2), and assists (7.0) per game entering Wednesday. Like Giddey, Sengun is a player known for stuffing box scores and one you should include in your lineups whenever possible. He had one of his worst performances of the season the first time he saw Phoenix, though he is in a good spot to bounce back after a string of strong performances since. He has scored 19 or more points in seven of the eight games following that loss to the Suns, adding three double-doubles and one triple-double in that span.

Sengun’s 21.9 PPG across his first 16 games would be a career high over a full season for the sixth-year big man. His efficiency has been the most impressive part of this hot start, as the Turkish international is shooting 55.1% from the field and 50% from three on low volume. He is also supplementing his scoring with 6.4 free-throw attempts per game, rewarded for his low-post work early this season. While Sengun is one of the best scoring bigs in the game, his willingness to pass and create for others makes him an elite fantasy option on most nights.

[![Image 78](https://go.metabet.io/03325fb169d4ca908f52c07a4505bb8f/img/logo-watermark.svg)](https://www.metabet.io/?utm_source=lineups&utm_medium=adtile-watermark&utm_campaign=nba/jonathan_kuminga/player_147568 "Powered by MetaBet")[](https://www.metabet.io/odds)

We round out our lineup with the Wolves’ big-name summer acquisition, who has quickly found his footing with his new squad. Former Hawks F Jonathan Kuminga has been a steady force for Finch and the Timberwolves this season, averaging a healthy 13.4 PPG with solid defense and rebounding to go along with it. He has been particularly strong over Minnesota’s last three games, averaging 18.0 PPG on an impressive 54.1% shooting. Kuminga should continue to see a steady number of shots with Edwards out and ranks fourth on the team with 10.4 FGA per game.

He and the Timberwolves face the Thunder for the first time this season, with the 2025 NBA champions running very thin in the frontcourt. C Aday Mara, C Chet Holmgren, and PF Jaylin Williams are all ruled out Wednesday, giving Kuminga a solid opportunity to dominate the interior. 8.8 of his field goal attempts per game come inside the arc, an area Oklahoma City has defended well this season but not in recent games. The Thunder have allowed the second-fewest points in the paint per game (39.4) across 18 games but have seen that number balloon to 51.3 over their last three.

## The Pick: Spurs 114, Trail Blazers 111

We’ll take the Under based on head-to-head data and Portland’s home stats.

## Game Betting Analysis

The data is screaming at the Under for this one, so we’ll abide and dig into the trends. Let’s start with head-to-head data as the Under has hit in eight of nine meetings between San Antonio and Portland. Both teams enter with Under records this season as well, the Spurs owning a 6-11-1 O/U mark and Portland’s 7-10 combine for a 13-21-1 O/U mark that favors the Under. Portland is also 1-4 to the Under when playing inside the Moda Center this season, averaging about 222 points per game. While San Antonio has won 14 straight games against Western Conference teams, the spread is a low -2.5-points and the Spurs have covered four straight games against the Blazers, so the Spurs’ ATS market is definitely on the table as well. Our only issue there is that Portland is 11-6 on the ATS this season compared to San Antonio’s 8-10 ATS mark – so we’ll stick to the Under for this one.

* * *

## Spurs-Trail Blazers Odds (Spread, Moneyline, Over/Under)

## Deciding Factor: San Antonio’s League-Best Net Rating vs. Portland’s Backcourt Stripped Down to Deni Avdija

San Antonio brings the NBA’s best record (15-3, first in the West) into Moda Center on Wednesday, and the margin behind that mark is just as telling as the record itself: a plus-9.4 scoring differential (112.8 points scored, 103.4 allowed), an 8-0 mark on the road, and only one loss in their last 16 games. What makes it scarier for Portland is that the Spurs just proved their depth can cover for their stars — Victor Wembanyama (lower back tightness) and De’Aaron Fox (right shin contusion) both sat out Monday’s win at Sacramento, San Antonio’s first missed game of the season for Wembanyama, and the team won anyway behind Stephon Castle’s 28 points and 11 assists, Julian Champagnie’s 22 points and Luke Kornet’s 18-and-11. Castle is averaging 16.6 points and 6.2 assists on the season but 9.0 assists over his last five games, evidence the offense keeps humming even without its two headline stars, and San Antonio’s 118.6 offensive rating and 106.3 defensive rating both outpace Portland’s marks (116.8 and 110.4) by a wide margin. If Wembanyama (29.9 points, 12.3 rebounds, 54.1% shooting when healthy) and Fox are both able to suit up Wednesday on top of that depth, San Antonio is positioned to extend its perfect road record to 9-0.

Portland, meanwhile, sits at 11-6 and seventh in the West, but its backcourt has been stripped down to almost nothing beyond Deni Avdija: Ja Morant remains out until at least December 1 with a Grade 2 hamstring strain suffered October 29, Shaedon Sharpe is done until at least March 2 following surgery for a torn meniscus, and Vit Krejci is out into mid-January after fracturing his left ankle and undergoing surgery on November 11 — leaving Chauncey Billups without three of his intended perimeter pieces. That void has forced Avdija into an outsized workload, and he’s answered with 24.3 points a game on the season and 27.0 over his last five, including a 31-point outing at Philadelphia, with rookie center Donovan Clingan (11.6 points, 11.3 rebounds, 59.4% shooting) the only other consistently featured option. The results at home have been uneven — Portland is just 3-2 at Moda Center with both losses by more than 20 points — and the Blazers have dropped three of their last five overall. If San Antonio’s stars play through their minor ailments and Castle keeps distributing the way he did in Sacramento, the Spurs’ top-to-bottom depth against a Blazers team leaning on one healthy shot creator gives San Antonio a real path to covering a number as large as -4.5 on the road.

* * *

## The Pick: Thunder 113, Timberwolves 112

We’ll take the Minnesota spread based on head-to-head data, Minnesota’s road success and the Thunder’s current ATS struggles.

## Game Betting Analysis

It’s hard not to take Minnesota here for many reason, but the elephant in the room is the best ATS mark in the entire NBA with a 14-4 ATS record and doing so without Anthony Edwards for most of it. Minnesota has covered the spread in five of seven games against Oklahoma City, but only won outright in the last five encounters played in Oklahoma City. The Timberwolves are on a 6-0 ATS run when playing on the road while Oklahoma City is also in the middle of a 1-7 ATS skid over its last eight games. Finally, Minnesota is also on a 7-0 outright run over its last seven games against the Western Conference. We’ll skip the total as the data isn’t the clearest for this game, and land with Minnesota to cover – if you want to take the outright road underdog feel free, but we have reservations with OKC’s home success against the Wolves.

* * *

## Timberwolves-Thunder Odds (Spread, Moneyline, Over/Under)

## Deciding Factor: Minnesota’s No-Excuses Depth Without Edwards vs. Oklahoma City’s Rotation Decimated by Injury

Minnesota arrives in Oklahoma City on Wednesday at 13-5 and third in the West despite having played almost the entire season without its best player — Anthony Edwards, who had knee surgery and has appeared in just four games, is now looking at a January return rather than the original six-to-eight-week recovery window, with The Athletic’s Jon Krawczynski reporting Edwards is “off of the crutches now” but still “very much in the beginning stages of getting back out there.” The Wolves haven’t needed him to roll: they’ve won two straight, including a 142-116 blow-up of Brooklyn on Monday in which Jaden McDaniels dropped 34 points, 10 rebounds and 6 assists, and they’re carrying that balance into Oklahoma City at 6-0 against the spread on the road this season. Four different Timberwolves are averaging in double figures with real complementary skill sets — McDaniels at 18.4 a game, Ayo Dosunmu at 18.2, LaMelo Ball at 17.2 points with 5.6 assists and 3.2 made threes (10th in the NBA), and Jonathan Kuminga chipping in 13.4 points and 5.4 rebounds since joining the rotation — with Rudy Gobert still anchoring the glass at 10.6 boards a night. That kind of four-man scoring floor is exactly the sort of attack that travels well against a shorthanded opponent, which is precisely what Minnesota is walking into Wednesday.

Oklahoma City, meanwhile, is 10-8 and favored at home (-2.5, -146 on the moneyline) largely on the strength of Shai Gilgeous-Alexander’s monster season (32.1 points, 4.6 assists per game), but the roster around him has been gutted by long-term injuries well beyond the typical day-to-day bumps: Chet Holmgren (knee) isn’t expected back until at least January 2, Jalen Williams (leg) and Cason Wallace (shoulder) are both out until at least March 2, and Thomas Sorber (knee) is shelved into late January. On top of those season-altering absences, Wednesday’s report lists four more Thunder regulars as game-time decisions — Alex Caruso (hip), Jaylin Williams (back), Aday Mara (lower leg) and Nikola Topic (hip) — which could leave Mark Daigneault piecing together his rotation almost by committee around Isaiah Hartenstein (8.4 points, 10.6 rebounds) and Ajay Mitchell (16.7 points, 47.1% shooting). If several of those game-time calls go against Oklahoma City and Minnesota’s balanced four-man scoring attack keeps clicking the way it has away from Target Center, the Wolves have a real path to covering as road underdogs and exposing just how thin this Thunder roster has become behind its MVP-caliber star.

* * *

## The Pick: Heat 119, 76ers 118

We’ll take the Miami ATS based on head-to-head data along with road team statistics for the Sixers.

## Game Betting Analysis

The Heat have a lot of data to support them in this game, beginning with their current seven-game winning streak. Miami enters this game with its last loss on November 5 against Golden State, beating Cleveland, Detroit, Houston and Oklahoma City after. The Heat have also owned the head-to-head record with outright wins in six of seven meetings, while covering the spread in six of the last eight against Philadelphia. Philly has also seen the road team cover the spread in five straight games. With the total data murky, we’ll stick with the Heat and the various data points suggesting they cover – possibly win.

* * *

## Heat-76ers Odds (Spread, Moneyline, Over/Under)

## Deciding Factor: Antetokounmpo’s Two-Way Workload vs. Joel Embiid’s Game-Time Decision

Miami comes into Wednesday’s 5 p.m. tip at Xfinity Mobile Arena at 10-6 and as road underdogs (+3.5, +132 on the moneyline), but the engine driving this team is putting up numbers that look more like a top-three MVP campaign than a typical fourth-place-in-scoring line. Giannis Antetokounmpo is averaging 28.2 points, 12.6 rebounds and 5.5 assists on a scorching 60.2% from the field — fifth-best in the NBA in field-goal percentage — while doing it all while managing a day-to-day groin issue that has him listed with an expected return date of November 25, tonight’s game. Bam Adebayo is playing the ideal complementary two-way role alongside him, averaging 19.2 points, 8.2 rebounds, 3.7 assists and 2.1 steals (fifth in the league), giving Miami a frontcourt pairing that can dominate the glass and protect the rim at the same time Philadelphia’s own interior depth is being tested. If Giannis is anywhere near full health tonight, Miami has the one matchup advantage — size and two-way force in the paint — capable of flipping a game where the Sixers are favored on paper.

Philadelphia, meanwhile, leads the East at 14-4 behind Tyrese Maxey (25.7 points, 5.6 assists, 36.4% from three, eighth in the NBA in scoring) and new arrivals Jaylen Brown (22.4 points, 49.8% shooting, 38.4% from deep) and LeBron James (16.6 points, 6.7 rebounds, 6.5 assists), but the frontcourt behind that perimeter talent is precarious entering this one: Joel Embiid is a game-time decision due to illness, and backup big Ariel Hukporti is already out for the season with an Achilles injury, leaving the Sixers with almost no proven size insurance if Embiid can’t go or has to be managed on a restriction. Philadelphia has also been shaky at home this season against physical opponents, going just 4-6 against the spread there, even as Miami carries its own warning sign on the road (1-6 ATS away from home this year). Still, if Embiid sits or is limited and Giannis keeps playing at this level on both ends, Miami has a real path to exploiting Philadelphia’s suddenly thin middle and pulling off the road upset as a field-goal underdog.

* * *

We’ll jump on the first game of the day, a late-afternoon clash between the Heat and 76ers in Philadelphia. Maxey checks into the favorable matchup averaging exactly 34.5 points + rebounds + assists per game on the season, while also shooting 40.0% from three-point range at home. The dynamic veteran is still sporting a formidable 25.8% usage rate despite having gained a pair of highly prominent teammates in Jaylen Brown and LeBron James this past offseason, and Wednesday, he should be in line for more opportunity considering Joel Embiid is doubtful due to an illness.

Maxey sports a 29.3% usage rate – the second highest on the Sixers behind Jaylen Brown – with Embiid off the floor this season, and he’s also averaging 35.4 points + rebounds + assists per 36 minutes in that scenario. Miami has also been a boon for point-guard production all season, allowing an NBA-high 33.6 offensive efficiency rating to the position, along with 48.8% shooting, including 42.9% from three-point range. The Heat is also conceding the fourth-most points + rebounds + assists per game (201.3), furthering Maxey’s prospects in a game where Philly has a robust 120.25-point implied team total.

**Original Line Publish:** 34.5

[![Image 43](https://go.metabet.io/03325fb169d4ca908f52c07a4505bb8f/img/logo-watermark.svg)](https://www.metabet.io/?utm_source=lineups&utm_medium=adtile-watermark&utm_campaign=nba/coby_white/player_132775 "Powered by MetaBet")[](https://www.metabet.io/odds)

White has been progressively ramping up since making his season debut Nov. 2, boosting his both his production and minutes over time. He’s close to hitting on all cylinders nine games in, as he just played a season-high 28 minutes on Sunday against the Grizzlies and tied a season best with 21 points in that span. White is averaging a solid 18.6 points, 3.8 assists and 2.6 rebounds across 24 minutes over his last five games, a span where he’s encouragingly putting up 18.8 field-goal + free-throw attempts per contest.

White now gets a pre-Thanksgiving crack at his old Bulls squad, a team that’s been highly targetable with Overs all season. Chicago is ranked No. 28 in offensive efficiency rating allowed to point guards (31.6), also surrendering 47.2% shooting, including 41.6% from behind the arc. The Bulls are also averaging an NBA-high 107.7 possessions per game while allowing an Eastern Conference-high 212.2 points + rebounds + assists per road contest, all numbers that further boost White’s prospects.

**Original Line Publish**: 23.5

[![Image 49](https://go.metabet.io/03325fb169d4ca908f52c07a4505bb8f/img/logo-watermark.svg)](https://www.metabet.io/?utm_source=lineups&utm_medium=adtile-watermark&utm_campaign=nba/ty_jerome/player_132853 "Powered by MetaBet")[](https://www.metabet.io/odds)

Jerome is authoring a career-best season, averaging a stellar 25.1 points, 5.2 assists and 3.5 rebounds per contest while shooting an impressive 47.9% overall. The breakout veteran already has six tallies of 27 points or better, seven four-rebound-plus efforts and nine games with five assists or more. He’s been far and away at his best at home, where he’s put up 39.0 points + rebounds + assists across 31.6 minutes per game, a sample where he’s also shot a blistering 57.6%, including 39.5% from behind the arc.

Jerome comes into Wednesday on an eight-game run of strong production, recording 33.0 points + rebounds + assists over 30.4 minutes per contest in that span while putting up 22.1 field-goal + free-throw attempts per game. He’s poised to continue seeing highly elevated usage Wednesday, as Cameron Boozer (ankle) remains out and Jerome boasts a team-high 37.5% usage rate without his star rookie teammate on the floor. Jerome is also averaging 43.3 points + rebounds + assists per 36 minutes in that scenario, and he’s facing a Clippers squad that’s ranked No. 26 in offensive efficiency rating allowed to point guards (30.1) and is conceding the seventh-most points + rebounds + assists per road game (203.5).

**Original Line Publish:**33.5

Let’s dive into three player props that catch our eye for Tuesday’s action. Be sure to[check out the NBA betting trends page](https://www.lineups.com/nba/odds/betting-trends/)for odds, data and insights on every game.

## **Best NBA Tuesday Props**

*   Paul George Over 23.5 Points + Rebounds
*   Kyrie Irving Over 26.5 Points + Assists

### **Paul George (Celtics vs. Wizards) Over Points + Rebounds**

George is off the injury report for Tuesday’s game after getting Sunday’s loss to the Warriors off for rest. The veteran forward has quickly proven a good fit in Boston, where he’s averaging 19.9 points (on 40.7% three-point shooting), 5.6 rebounds, 3.8 assists and 2.2 steals per contest over 30.1 minutes per game. George is averaging 18.9 field-goal + free-throw attempts per game as well, so his usage remains very encouraging despite sharing the floor with Jayson Tatum.

The Wizards are solid targets for the veteran forward, as Washington is allowing the sixth-most points per home game (118.8) and the third-most points + rebounds per game in that split as well (175.0). George has also been at his best on the road, where he’s putting up 27.2 points + rebounds on 46.2% shooting, including 40.6% three-point shooting, across 29.1 minutes per contest. He’s 7-5 to the Over on the 23.5 over his last 12 as well, and he finished on the hook with 23 in one other instance.

**Original Line Publish:** 23.5

### **Kyrie Irving (Mavericks vs. Nuggets) Over Points + Assists**

Irving has looked good in his first 14 games back from his 2024-25 ACL tear, averaging 22.0 points, 4.6 assists, 3.6 rebounds and 1.2 steals per contest over 30.7 minutes per game. As the latter figure reflects, Irving is being handled with some caution, but he’s proven an effective complement to Cooper Flagg and is averaging a solid 20.5 field-goal + free-throw attempts per contest. The talented point guard now faces a Nuggets team that’s been more vulnerable defensively than in recent years and should ensure a competitive matchup where Irving should see plenty of minutes.

Denver is ranked No. 25 in offensive efficiency rating allowed to point guards (29.5), along with allowing a Western Conference-high 10.4 assists per contest to the position. The Nuggets are also surrendering over eight more points per game at home (119.3) – on 49.0% shooting – than on the road (111.3), and Denver is also surrendering the fourth-most points + assists per game in that split (148.4). Then, Irving has been particularly aggressive on the road, where he’s putting up 9.9 three-point attempts per contest.

**Original Line Publish**: 26.5

# NBA Player Props Today: Scottie Barnes, James Harden and Fred VanVleet Best Bets

 Our best NBA player prop picks for games scheduled today with plays on Scottie Barnes, James Harden and Fred VanVleet.

Let’s dive into three player props that catch our eye for Monday’s action. Be sure to[check out the NBA betting trends page](https://www.lineups.com/nba/odds/betting-trends/)for odds, data and insights on every game.

## **Best NBA Monday Props**

*   Scottie Barnes Over 19.5 Points
*   James Harden Over 25.5 Points + Assists
*   Fred VanVleet Over 14.5 Points + Assists

### **Scottie Barnes (Raptors vs. Bucks) Over Points**

Barnes is putting together a career-best season that includes a career-best average of 24.3 points, a figure over four points a game better than his prior high-water mark of 19.9 points in the 2023-24 season. The highly versatile forward is shooting a crisp 49.5% as well, and Barnes boasts a massive, team-high 34.1% usage rate and average of 30.8 points per 36 minutes with RJ Barrett (hamstring), who’ll remain sidelined Monday, off the floor.

Barnes has recorded at least 20 points in nine of 10 games this season, a sample where he’s shot 52.8%. The opposing Bucks have been highly targetable all season for Overs, and they’re surrendering an Eastern Conference-high 30.9 offensive efficiency rating to power forwards. Milwaukee is also conceding 51.4% shooting in the last three games, and the eighth-most made three-pointers per road game (15.0) on the season.

Given Barnes’ current usage and his track record against the 19.5, this prop is in play for a game where Toronto carries a solid 117-point implied total.

**Original Line Publish:** 19.5

### **James Harden (Cavaliers vs. Bulls) Over Points + Assists**

Harden has admittedly been a tough player to figure out this season, with the most recent evidence of such coming with his vintage 51-point outburst over 40 minutes against the Pacers on Friday. Harden does have 20- and 32-point tallies in two other November games as well, however, and despite a forgettable 3-for-10 showing from the field in his first meeting with the Bulls three games ago, he still mustered 19 points + assists.

Given Chicago’s pace of play and defensive struggles this season, not to mention some of Harden’s recent performances, banking on a slight improvement in the rematch isn’t the most outlandish bet to make. The Bulls are averaging an NBA-high 108.1 possessions per game and surrendering the third-highest offensive efficiency rating to point guards (32.4), along with 48.0% shooting, including 42.3% from three-point range. Chicago is also allowing a league-high 29.1 assists per game, and Harden is supplying 32.6 points + assists across the last four games.

Finally, it’s worth noting Cleveland’s slate-high 124.5-point implied team total, which only serves to add to Harden’s appeal.

**Original Line Publish**: 25.5

### **Fred VanVleet (Rockets vs. Clippers) Over Points + Assists**

VanVleet has been productive in his first 14 games back from a lost 2025-26 season due to an ACL tear, as the veteran guard is averaging 14.4 points, 5.7 assists, 2.9 rebounds and 1.1 steals per contest. VanVleet is also draining his 7.3 three-point attempts per game at a 39.2% clip, the second-highest figure of his career in that category. VanVleet is averaging 17.6 points + assists per home game, where he’s been even better from long range with a 40.0% success rate.

The Clippers have been a team to target with point guard Overs all season, as Los Angeles is allowing the fourth-highest offensive efficiency rating to ones (31.1) and the third-most points per game (31.8) and 47.4% shooting to the position. Then, the Clips are allowing the fifth-most assists per road game (29.7), as well as a Western Conference-high 38.7% success rate from behind the arc in that split as well.

VanVleet is also averaging 21.0 points + assists with 44.8% three-point shooting in the last eight games, and Monday’s matchup suggests he could keep that strong stretch going.

**Original Line Publish:**14.5

Murray is putting together another stat-sheet-stuffing season in the early going, already averaging 24.9 points with the help of career-best 50.2% shooting, including a career-high 46.2% success rate from behind the arc. The veteran point guard is averaging 35.7 points + rebounds + assists per game at a clip of 0.98 PRA per minute on the floor. Murray has been at his most productive on the road as well, where he’s amassed 36.3 PRA at a clip of what amounts to 1.00 PRA per minute.

The matchup Sunday gives Murray a good chance of delivering one of his higher-percentile efforts. The Bulls continue to play at a league-high pace (107.6 possessions per game) and have been highly vulnerable to point guards, conceding the third-highest offensive efficiency rating to the position (31.3), along with 47.5% shooting, including 41.7% from three-point range. Chicago is also surrendering the seventh-most points (28.2) and fifth-most assists (9.6) per contest to ones, and Denver has an elevated 123.0-point implied team total for Sunday’s contest as well.

**Original Line Publish:** 33.5

### **Desmond Bane (Magic at Wizards) Over Points + Rebounds + Assists**

[](https://www.lineups.com/)

![Image 42](https://metabet.static.areyouwatchingthis.com/03325fb169d4ca908f52c07a4505bb8f/images/teams/80x80/10213.png)Desmond Bane

[![Image 43: Kalshi](https://go.metabet.io/03325fb169d4ca908f52c07a4505bb8f/img/sportsbooks/square/kalshi.svg)](https://lineups.go.metabet.io/bet/nba_mvp_finals/58143/kalshi_sc)[To Win Finals MVP](https://lineups.go.metabet.io/bet/nba_mvp_finals/58143/kalshi_sc)[1¢](https://lineups.go.metabet.io/bet/nba_mvp_finals/58143/kalshi_sc)

[![Image 44](https://go.metabet.io/03325fb169d4ca908f52c07a4505bb8f/img/logo-watermark.svg)](https://www.metabet.io/?utm_source=lineups&utm_medium=adtile-watermark&utm_campaign=nba/desmond_bane/player_58143 "Powered by MetaBet")[](https://www.metabet.io/odds)

Bane hasn’t been quite as productive so far this season as he was during his 2025-26 Magic debut campaign, but the veteran wing is still providing a solid 18.6 points, 4.1 rebounds, 2.7 assists and 1.2 steals per contest. Bane has shot just 42.5% overall, which is easily a career-low figure, but his 42.9% success rate from deep is his best figure since the 2021-22 season.

Bane has been markedly more productive and efficient in recent games, however, supplying 21.2 points (on 52.9% shooting, including 54.9% from deep), 4.2 rebounds, 3.2 assists and 2.0 steals per contest over his last five games. Two of those contests came in tough defensive matchups against the 76ers and Celtics, and his Sunday matchup pits him against a Wizards team he produced 26 PRA against over 36 minutes on Nov. 2, and that’s allowed the seventh-highest offensive efficiency rating to small forwards (24.2), along with 34.8 PRA per game to the position.

**Original Line Publish**: 27.5

[![Image 49](https://go.metabet.io/03325fb169d4ca908f52c07a4505bb8f/img/logo-watermark.svg)](https://www.metabet.io/?utm_source=lineups&utm_medium=adtile-watermark&utm_campaign=nba/darryn_peterson/player_276494 "Powered by MetaBet")[](https://www.metabet.io/odds)

Peterson is starting to hit his stride at the NBA level after a lackluster first seven games of his debut season. The rookie second overall pick has gone off for averages of 20.3 points, 4.9 rebounds, 4.1 assists and 1.2 steals per contest in his next nine contests, and he just contributed his first career double-double Friday against the Suns. He’s also boosted his offensive usage over the latter span, putting up 16.2 field-goal attempts per contest after averaging 13.1 in the first seven contests.

Dallas is allowing the second-highest shooting percentage to two-guards (49.8%), including 37.5% three-point shooting to the position. Then, Peterson finished under 18.5 points in each of his first seven games of the season, but since then, the talented rookie has gone 6-3 to the Over on the 18.5 points. With Sunday’s matchup sporting a tight 1.5-point spread and solid 225.5-point projected total – and the Mavs on the second night of a back-to-back – Peterson should get plenty of run and have a soild chance of cashing this prop.

**Original Line Publish:**18.5

For Alberta: 18+ only. Only play with operators authorised in your province. Gambling problem? Call the Addiction Helpline at 1-866-332-2322 (24/7). Please gamble responsibly.

For Ontario: 19+ only. Only play with operators authorised in your province. Gambling problem? Call ConnexOntario at 1-866-531-2600 (24/7). Please gamble responsibly.

Affiliate disclosure: Some links may earn us compensation. This doesn’t influence our editorial content. Offers and terms can change—always check the operator’s official terms.
